. Then, we have,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{B}\leq\min(\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)\right]\right)^{M^{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{1\leq\ell\leq M^{2}}\mathsf{B}_{\ell}\leq\min(\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)\right]

(518)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M^{2}}\mathsf{B}_{\ell}\leq\min(\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)\right]

(519)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\bar{\mathsf{B}}\leq\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)\right],

(520)

where
\bar{\mathsf{B}}\triangleq\mathsf{Binomial}\left(M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|,\varphi\right)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{m=0}^{\min(\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\right]^{M^{2}}\leq(1+\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left[\bar{\mathsf{B}}\leq\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)\right]

(521)

\displaystyle=(1+\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\sum_{m=0}^{\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\varphi^{m}(1-\varphi)^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|-m}

(522)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m},

(523)

where in the last equality we have used the fact that
(1+\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}(1-\varphi)^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}=1
. Therefore, combining (
501
), (
502
), (
514
), and (
523
), we get that,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{L}_{n,\leq\mathsf{D}}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}^{2}\leq\prod_{i,j\in[M]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\min(\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{M^{2}}}.

(524)

Remark 2
.

As a sanity check, note that if we take
\mathsf{D}\to\infty
, we end up with,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{L}_{n,\leq\infty}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i,j\in[M]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{M^{2}}}

(525)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i,j\in[M]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(1+\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{M^{2}}},

(526)

which is the expression we got in our derivation of the statistical lower bound.

Let us simplify (
524
). Note that,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\min(\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\leq\mathsf{A}+\mathsf{B},

(527)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}\triangleq\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq\mathsf{D}\right\}\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m},

(528)

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}\triangleq\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq\mathsf{D}\right\}\cdot\left[1+\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\right].

(529)

As for
\mathsf{A}
, using the Binomial theorem, we readily see that,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}

\displaystyle=(1+\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}|}=(1+\lambda_{M}^{2})^{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}|},

(530)

for
\lambda_{M}^{2}=(1+\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}}-1
, and therefore

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}=\sum_{m=0}^{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M}^{2m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq\mathsf{D}\right\}.

(531)

Now, for
\mathsf{B}
, we split our analysis into two parts, where
\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
and
|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
, and start our analysis with the later. Our goal is to find a parameter
\bar{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}^{2m}.

(532)

To that end, on one hand, we will use the following observation. Let
f(\bar{m})\triangleq\binom{\bar{n}}{\bar{m}}\alpha^{\bar{m}}
, for
1\leq\bar{m}\leq\bar{k}
and
\alpha>0
. We would like to find the value of
\bar{m}
which maximizes
f(\bar{m})
in the interval
0\leq\bar{m}\leq\bar{k}
. We have,

\displaystyle\frac{f(\bar{m}-1)}{f(\bar{m})}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{\bar{m}}{\bar{n}-\bar{m}+1}.

(533)

Thus, we have
f(\bar{m}-1)\leq f(\bar{m})
for,

\displaystyle\bar{m}\leq\frac{\alpha(\bar{n}+1)}{1+\alpha}.

(534)

Therefore, the value
m^{\star}\triangleq\min\left(\left\lceil\frac{\alpha(\bar{n}+1)}{1+\alpha}\right\rceil,\bar{k}\right)
maximizes
f(\bar{m})
over
0\leq\bar{m}\leq\bar{k}
. Using this fact, in our case we note that,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m^{\star}}\lambda^{2m^{\star}},

(535)

where

\displaystyle m^{\star}=\min\left(\left\lceil\frac{\lambda^{2}(M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|+1)}{\lambda^{2}+1}\right\rceil,M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right).

(536)

Let us now assume that
\lambda^{2}=\Theta(1)
, and address the case where
\lambda=o(\mathsf{D}^{-2})
later. When
\lambda=\Theta(1)
we may assumed without loss of generality that
\lambda^{2}\geq 1
and thus
\lambda^{2}/(1+\lambda^{2})\geq 1/2
.
In the regime where
|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
, we have
m^{\star}=cM^{2}\mathsf{D}
, for some
0<c\leq 1
(in fact, since
M\geq 2
, under the assumption
|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
we have that
m^{\star}=M^{2}\mathsf{D}
and
c=1
). Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\cdot\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\lambda^{2cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}

(537)

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\cdot\left(\frac{eM^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\right)^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\lambda^{2cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}

(538)

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\cdot\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}(e\lambda^{2})^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}},

(539)

where we have used the fact that
\binom{n}{m}\leq\left(\frac{en}{m}\right)^{m}
, for
m\leq n
. On the other hand, for any
\bar{\lambda}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}^{2m}

\displaystyle\geq\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\bar{\lambda}^{2cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}

(540)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}M^{-2cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\bar{\lambda}^{2cM^{2}\mathsf{D}},

(541)

where we have used the fact that
\left(\frac{n}{m}\right)^{m}\leq\binom{n}{m}
, for
m\leq n
. Then, it can be checked that the term on the r.h.s. of (
541
) is larger than the term on the r.h.s. of (
539
) for any,

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}^{2}\geq\lambda^{2}_{M,2}\triangleq\left[M^{2}\mathsf{D}M^{2cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}(e\lambda^{2})^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\right]^{\frac{1}{cM^{2}D}}=eM^{2}[M^{2}D]^{\frac{1}{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}}\lambda^{2},

(542)

We mention here that
\bar{\lambda}^{2}
is bounded as
M
is assumed fixed and
c
is bounded away from
0
. To conclude, for
|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M,2}^{2m}.

(543)

For
\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
, we first show that there exists
\bar{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}^{M^{2}}\bar{\lambda}^{2m}.

(544)

This follows exactly from the same arguments above. Indeed, as in the previous case, there is a
0<c\leq 1
such that
m^{\star}=cM^{2}\mathsf{D}
such that the
m^{\star}
term is the maximal among the summands of the l.h.s. of (
544
), and therefore multiplying it by
M^{2}\mathsf{D}
upper bounds the l.h.s. of (
544
). If
c\mathsf{D}
is an integer, similarly to the previous case we have

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}^{M^{2}}\bar{\lambda}^{2m}

\displaystyle\geq\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}^{M^{2}}\bar{\lambda}^{2c\mathsf{D}}

(545)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\bar{\lambda}^{2c\mathsf{D}},

(546)

Thus, (
544
) holds for,

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}^{2}\geq\lambda_{M,3}^{2}\triangleq(M^{2}\mathsf{D})^{\frac{1}{c\mathsf{D}}}(e\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}}.

(547)

On the other hand, if
c\mathsf{D}
is not an integer, note that the function
f_{\hat{m}}(x)=\left(\frac{\hat{m}}{x}\lambda^{2}\right)^{M^{2}x}
has at most one extrema point in
(0,\hat{m}]
, at
\frac{\hat{m}}{e}
. Consider
\hat{m}=|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|
. In the regime where
\lambda^{2}=\Theta(1)
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\bar{\lambda}^{2}
is sufficiently large such that
\bar{\lambda}^{2}/(1+\bar{\lambda}^{2})
is arbitrarily close to one. Thus from the definition of
m^{\star}
and since we are in the regime where
|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq\mathsf{D}
, we can assume that
c
is bounded away from
e^{-1}
. In particular
[c\mathsf{D}-1,c\mathsf{D}+1]
does not contain the maximum of
f_{\hat{m}}
. Hence, it must hold that,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\bar{\lambda}^{2c\mathsf{D}}\leq\max\left(\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{\left\lceil c\mathsf{D}\right\rceil}\right)^{\left\lceil c\mathsf{D}\right\rceil M^{2}}\bar{\lambda}^{2\left\lceil c\mathsf{D}\right\rceil},\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{\left\lfloor c\mathsf{D}\right\rfloor}\right)^{\left\lfloor c\mathsf{D}\right\rfloor M^{2}}\bar{\lambda}^{2\left\lfloor c\mathsf{D}\right\rfloor}\right).

(548)

Thus, there exists
0<\tilde{c}\leq 1
such that
\tilde{c}\mathsf{D}\in\{\left\lfloor c\mathsf{D}\right\rfloor,\left\lceil c\mathsf{D}\right\rceil\}
and,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}^{M^{2}}\bar{\lambda}^{2m}

\displaystyle\geq\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{\tilde{c}\mathsf{D}}^{M^{2}}\bar{\lambda}^{2\tilde{c}\mathsf{D}}

(549)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{\tilde{c}\mathsf{D}}\right)^{\tilde{c}M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\bar{\lambda}^{2\tilde{c}\mathsf{D}}

(550)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\bar{\lambda}^{2c\mathsf{D}}.

(551)

In particular, (
544
) holds for the same
\overline{\lambda}^{2}
from (
547
).

Next, we show further that there exists
\tilde{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}^{M^{2}}\lambda_{M,3}^{2m}\leq\sum_{m=0}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\tilde{\lambda}^{2m},

(552)

Indeed, let
<0<c\leq 1
be such that
m^{\star}=c\mathsf{D}
maximizes the summands of the l.h.s. of (
552
). On one hand, we note that,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}^{M^{2}}\lambda_{M,3}^{2m}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{D}\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{M^{2}}\lambda_{M,3}^{2cD}

(553)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{D}\left(\frac{M^{2}D}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{M^{2}}\lambda_{M,3}^{2cD}

(554)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}\left(\frac{M^{2}}{c}\right)^{M^{2}}\lambda_{M,3}^{2cD},

(555)

where we have used the fact that we are in the regime where
\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\tilde{\lambda}^{2m}\geq\left(\frac{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{c\mathsf{D}}\right)^{c\mathsf{D}}\tilde{\lambda}^{2c\mathsf{D}}\geq\left(\frac{1}{c}\right)^{c\mathsf{D}}\tilde{\lambda}^{2c\mathsf{D}},

(556)

where we have used the fact that we are in the regime where
\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|
. Then, it can be checked that the term on the r.h.s. of (
556
) is larger than the term on the r.h.s. of (
555
) for any,

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}^{2}\geq\lambda^{2}_{M,4}\triangleq\left[c^{-c\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{D}\left(\frac{M^{2}}{c}\right)^{M^{2}}\lambda_{M,3}^{2cD}\right]^{\frac{1}{c\mathsf{D}}}=\frac{\mathsf{D}^{\frac{1}{c\mathsf{D}}}\left(\frac{M^{2}}{c}\right)^{\frac{M^{2}}{c\mathsf{D}}}}{c}\lambda_{M,3}^{2}=O(1).

(557)

Thus, combining (
544
) and (
552
), we get that for
\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\leq\sum_{m=0}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M,4}^{2m}.

(558)

Recalling the definition of
\mathsf{B}
in (
529
), we get from (
543
) and (
558
),

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq\mathsf{D}\right\}\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}

(559)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right\}\sum_{m=0}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M,4}^{2m}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right\}\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M,2}^{2m}.

(560)

Let
\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2}\triangleq\max(\lambda_{M}^{2},\lambda_{M,2}^{2},\lambda_{M,4}^{2})
. Then, from (
527
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\min(\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda^{2m}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{A}+\mathsf{B}\right]

(561)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M}^{2m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq\mathsf{D}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M,4}^{2m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\lambda_{M,2}^{2m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right\}\right]

(562)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{M^{2}\mathsf{D}}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|\geq M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right\}\right]

(563)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\leq 2\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2m}\right].

(564)

Substituting the above in (
524
), we finally obtain that,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{L}_{n,\leq\mathsf{D}}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}^{2}\leq 2\prod_{i,j\in[M]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|)}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2m}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{M^{2}}}.

(565)

Note that
\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2}
can be simplified as follows. We have,

\displaystyle\lambda_{M,2}^{2}=eM^{2}[M^{2}D]^{\frac{1}{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}}\lambda^{2}\leq 2eM^{2}\lambda^{2},

(566)

where we have used the fact that
[M^{2}D]^{\frac{1}{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}}\leq 2
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\lambda_{M,4}^{2}=\frac{\mathsf{D}^{\frac{1}{c\mathsf{D}}}\left(\frac{M^{2}}{c}\right)^{\frac{M^{2}}{c\mathsf{D}}}}{c}(M^{2}\mathsf{D})^{\frac{1}{c\mathsf{D}}}(e\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}}\leq C(e\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}},

(567)

for some universal
C>0
independent of
M
and
\mathsf{D}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2}\leq\max\left\{1,2eM^{2}\lambda^{2},\lambda_{M}^{2},C(e\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}}\right\}.

(568)

Let us now consider the case where
\lambda^{2}=o(\mathsf{D^{-2}})
. The analysis of this case remains almost the same, with some minor adjustments. First, we observe that
m^{\star}=cM^{2}\mathsf{D}
, which maximizes the l.h.s. of (
538
), can be as small as
1
. Therefore, the constant
c
in (
541
) can vary in the interval
[(M^{2}\mathsf{D})^{-1},1]
. In particular,
\bar{\lambda}_{M,2}^{2}
is bounded by,

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}_{M,2}\triangleq\left[M^{2}\mathsf{D}M^{2cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}(e\lambda^{2})^{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}\right]^{\frac{1}{cM^{2}D}}=eM^{2}[M^{2}D]^{\frac{1}{cM^{2}\mathsf{D}}}\lambda^{2}\leq eM^{4}\mathsf{D}\lambda^{2}.

(569)

Using similar arguments, our new bounds on
\lambda_{M,3}^{2}
and
\lambda_{M,4}^{2}
are bounded as,

\displaystyle\lambda_{M,3}^{2}\leq\left(eM^{2}\mathsf{D}\lambda^{2}\right)^{M^{2}}\quad\mathsf{and}\quad\lambda_{M,4}^{2}\leq\mathsf{D}M^{2}\left(eM^{2}\mathsf{D}^{2}\lambda^{2}\right)^{M^{2}}\left(M^{4}\mathsf{D}\right)^{M^{4}}.

(570)

Finally, we note that the inequality in (
548
) still holds true. Indeed, under the assumption that
\lambda^{2}=o(D^{-2})
, we have that
m^{\star}
, which maximizes (
544
), must be
1
. Since it is assumed
\mathsf{D}\leq|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}|\leq M^{2}\mathsf{D}
, we have, for sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle m^{\star}=\min\left(\left\lceil\frac{\lambda^{2}(M^{2}|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}|+1)}{\lambda^{2}+1}\right\rceil,M^{2}\mathsf{D}\right)=\left\lceil\frac{\lambda^{2}(M^{2}\cdot M^{2}\mathsf{D}+1)}{\lambda^{2}+1}\right\rceil=1.

(571)

In particular,
c
in that case must be
(M^{2}\mathsf{D})^{-1}
, which is also bounded away from
e^{-1}
, that value that maximizes
f_{\hat{m}}
. Therefore,
(
548
)
holds.

We are now in a position to prove (
447
). Let
C>0
be the smallest constant for which Theorem
15
and Lemma
14
hold simultaneously. Also, define
\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\triangleq\left(\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2}M^{4}\mathsf{D}^{2}\right)^{\sqrt{M^{2}\mathsf{D}/2}}
. For
i=j
, using (
415
) in Theorem
15
, we know that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}|)}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2m}\right]=O(1),

(572)

provided that,

\displaystyle\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\cdot\max\left(\tau_{i}d_{i},s_{i}\right)}{n-k_{i}}\leq C.

(573)

This, however, is satisfied when (
447
) holds because,

\displaystyle\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\cdot\max\left(\tau_{i}d_{i},d^{2}_{i}\right)}{n-k_{i}}\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq M}\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\cdot\max\left(\tau_{j}d_{j},d^{2}_{j}\right)}{n-k_{j}}\leq C.

(574)

Next, for
i\neq j
, we note that,

\displaystyle\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\max\left(\sqrt{\tau_{i}d_{i}\tau_{j}d_{j}},d_{i}d_{j}\right)}{n-\min\left(k_{i},k_{j}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\max(\sqrt{\tau_{i}\tau_{j}d_{i}d_{j}},d_{i}d_{j})}{n-\min(k_{i},k_{j})}

(575)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\max(\tau_{i}d_{i},\tau_{j}d_{j},d_{i}^{2},d_{j}^{2})}{n-\min(k_{i},k_{j})}

(576)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\ell=i,j}\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\max(\tau_{\ell}d_{\ell},d_{\ell}^{2})}{n-k_{\ell}}

(577)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq\ell\leq M}\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\max(\tau_{\ell}d_{\ell},d_{\ell}^{2})}{n-k_{\ell}}\leq C,

(578)

where the last equality is due to (
447
), and therefore, by Lemma
14
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{\min(M^{2}\mathsf{D},|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}|)}\binom{|\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{j}|}{m}\bar{\lambda}^{2m}\right]=O(1).

(579)

Thus, we proved that under (
447
) each term in the product on the r.h.s. of (
565
) is bounded, and since
M
is finite, the entire product of (
565
) is bounded as well. The proof of (
448
) follows from the exact same arguments as above by using (
416
) in Theorem
15
Lemma
14
, and (
453
).

∎

6.4

Implications

The computational lower bounds in Theorem
15
provide general conditions under which
\left\|\mathsf{L}_{n,\leq\mathsf{D}}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}=O(1)
. While these conditions are formulated in terms of the vertex cover number, our upper bounds (given by the performances of the count test and the maximum degree test) are formulated in terms of
|e(\Gamma)|
and
d_{\max}(\Gamma)
solely. In this subsection, we bridge this gap for the dense and sparse regimes.

Theorem 16
.

Let
\mathsf{D}=(\mathsf{D}_{n})_{n}
be a sequence such that
\mathsf{D}=O(\log n)
, and let
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of graphs.

1.

Assuming that
\lambda^{2}=\chi^{2}(p||q)=\Theta(1)
, if

\displaystyle\max\left(|e(\Gamma)|,d_{\max}^{2}(\Gamma)\right)\leq n^{1-\varepsilon},

(580)

for some
\varepsilon>0
, we have
\left\|\mathsf{L}_{n,\leq\mathsf{D}}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}=O(1)
.

2.

Assume that
\lambda^{2}=\Theta(n^{-\alpha})
, where
0<\alpha<2
, and that
\Gamma
is an
(\epsilon,\delta,\zeta)
-polynomial family with
|v(\Gamma)|=\Theta(n^{\beta})
, for
0<\beta<1
. Then,
\left\|\mathsf{L}_{n,\leq\mathsf{D}}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}=O(1)
if

\displaystyle\beta<\min\left(\frac{2+\alpha}{2\varepsilon},\frac{1+\alpha}{2\delta}\right).

(581)

Proof of Theorem
16
.

From Proposition
3
we know that there exists a decomposition
\Gamma=\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{M}\Gamma_{\ell}
, for which (
168
) holds, and we take,

\displaystyle M_{n}=\left\lceil\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right\rceil.

(582)

By Proposition
6
strong detection is impossible if,

\displaystyle\max_{\ell=1,\dots,M}\left(\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})}{n-|v(\Gamma_{\ell})|}\cdot\max\left(\tau(\Gamma_{\ell})\cdot d_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell}),d_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell})^{2}\right)\right)\leq C,

(583)

where
\nu(M,\mathsf{D})\triangleq\left(\max(\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2},1)\cdot 2M^{4}\mathsf{D}^{2}\right)^{\sqrt{M^{2}\mathsf{D}/2}}
. Now, by (
168
), we have,

\displaystyle\max_{\ell=1,\dots,M}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})}{n-|v(\Gamma_{\ell})|}\cdot\max\left(\tau(\Gamma_{\ell})\cdot d_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell}),d_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell})^{2}\right)\right)

(584)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})}{n-|v(\Gamma)|}\cdot\max\left(|e(\Gamma)|\cdot d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{\frac{1}{M_{n}}},d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{2}\right)

(585)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})}{\delta n}\cdot\max\left(|e(\Gamma)|\cdot d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{\frac{1}{M_{n}}},d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{2}\right)

(586)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\nu(M,\mathsf{D})}{\delta n^{1-\frac{1}{M_{n}}}}\cdot\max\left(|e(\Gamma)|,d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{2}\right)

(587)

where the first equality follows from the fact that
|v(\Gamma_{\ell})|\leq|v(\Gamma)|
, for all
\ell\in[M]
, the second equality is because
|v(\Gamma)|\leq(1-\delta)n
, for some fixed
\delta>0
, the third equality follows from
d_{\max}(\Gamma)\leq|v(\Gamma)|
, and the last equality is because
|v(\Gamma)|\leq n
. Since for a fixed
M
and
\mathsf{D}=O(\log n)
, we have
\nu(M,\mathsf{D})=n^{o(1)}
, we finally get that if,

\displaystyle\max\left(|e(\Gamma)|,d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{2}\right)\leq n^{1-\varepsilon},

(588)

then (
583
) is satisfied for a sufficiently large
n
(regardless of the constant
C
), which proves (
580
).

Next, we follow the lines of the proof of Theorem
9
for the proving the condition (
581
) in the sparse regime. Let

\displaystyle 2\rho\triangleq\beta-\min\left(,\frac{1+\alpha}{2\delta},\frac{2+\alpha}{2\epsilon}\right)>0,

(589)

and let
\Gamma
be decomposed as

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Gamma_{M}
, where
M=\left\lceil\rho^{-1}\right\rceil
, such that for all
1\leq\ell\leq M
, we have

\displaystyle\tau(\Gamma_{\ell})d_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell})

\displaystyle\leq|e(\Gamma)|d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{\frac{1}{M}}\leq n^{\beta(\epsilon+\rho)+o(1)},

(590)

where such a decomposition exists due to Proposition
3
. By Proposition
6
,
\left\|\mathsf{L}_{n,\leq\mathsf{D}}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}
is bounded if

\displaystyle\max_{\ell=1,\dots,M}\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{M}\mathsf{D}\cdot\max\left(\tau(\Gamma_{\ell})d_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell}),d^{2}_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell})\bar{\lambda}_{M}\mathsf{D}\right)}{n-|v(\Gamma_{\ell})|}\leq C,

(591)

where

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}_{M}^{2}\triangleq\max\left((1+\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}}-1,eM^{4}\mathsf{D}\lambda^{2},(eM^{2}\mathsf{D}\lambda^{2})^{M^{2}},\mathsf{D}M^{2}\left(eM^{2}\mathsf{D}^{2}\lambda^{2}\right)^{M^{2}}\left(M^{4}\mathsf{D}\right)^{M^{4}}\right).

(592)

Note that under the assumption that
\lambda^{2}=\Theta(n^{-\alpha})
,
M
is finite, and
\mathsf{D}=O(\log(n))
, we also have that
\bar{\lambda}^{2}_{M}=n^{-\alpha+o(1)}
. Thus, from the definition of an
(\epsilon,\delta,\zeta)
-polynomial sequence, and since
|v(\Gamma)|=\Theta(n^{\beta})
and
\mathsf{D}=O(\log(n))=n^{o(1)}
for all
\ell
,

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{M}\mathsf{D}\cdot\max\left(\tau(\Gamma_{\ell})d_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell}),d^{2}_{\max}(\Gamma_{\ell})\bar{\lambda}_{M}\mathsf{D}\right)}{n-|v(\Gamma_{\ell})|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+o(1)}\max\left(n^{\epsilon\beta+\rho},n^{2\delta\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}+o(1)}\right)}{n^{1-o(1)}}

(593)

\displaystyle=n^{\max\left(\beta\epsilon+\rho-1-\frac{\alpha}{2},2\beta\delta-1-\alpha\right)}+o(1).

(594)

Note that by (
592
) we have

\displaystyle\max\left(\beta\epsilon+\rho-1-\frac{\alpha}{2},2\beta\delta-1-\alpha\right)<n^{-\rho},

(595)

and theretofore (
594
) decays to zero. In particular, the condition in (
591
) holds.
∎

We conclude this section by pointing out that the bounds in Theorem
16
are tight. Indeed, by Theorem
5
, in the dense regime, using the count and maximum degree tests (which runs in polynomial time), strong detection is possible whenever,

\displaystyle\max\left(|e(\Gamma)|,d_{\max}(\Gamma)^{2}\right)\geq n^{1+o(1)}.

(596)

In the sparse regime, under the setting of Theorem
16
, detection using the count and the maximum degree tests is possible when,

\displaystyle\beta>\min\left(\frac{2+\alpha}{2\varepsilon},\frac{1+\alpha}{2\delta}\right).

(597)

These complement our computational lower bounds. Furthermore, the statistical and computational bounds in Theorem
3
imply that a statistical-computational gap exist if and only if
\Gamma
has a positive polynomial density, i.e.,
\zeta>0
. Indeed, we observe that as long as
\alpha>0
, the line
\frac{\mu}{\alpha}
is strictly below the lines
\frac{2+\alpha}{2\epsilon}
and
\frac{1+\alpha}{2\delta}
in the region

\displaystyle\alpha\in\left(0,\min\left(\frac{2\zeta}{2\epsilon-\zeta},\frac{\zeta}{2\delta-\mu}\right)\right)\triangleq I_{\epsilon,\delta,\zeta}.

(598)

In particular, for any
\alpha\in I_{\epsilon,\delta,\zeta}
, and
\frac{\alpha}{\zeta}<\beta<\min(\frac{1+\alpha}{2\delta},\frac{2+\alpha}{2\epsilon})
, detection is possible (using the scan test), and by Theorem
16
, detection in polynomial time is impossible.

Appendix A

Additional Proofs

A.1

Essential number of planted edges

Let us show that strong detection is information-theoretically impossible if
|p-q|\cdot|e(\Gamma)|<c<1
. We already saw that the optimal risk satisfies (see, (
91
)),

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}^{\star}

\displaystyle=1-d_{\mathsf{TV}}({\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{0}},{\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{1}}).

(599)

Recall the following two facts about the total-variation distance (see, e.g.,
[
PW25
, Theorem 7.5]
and
[
MW15b
, Lemma 7]
).

1.

Convexity
:
(P,Q)\mapsto d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P,Q)
is a jointly convex function.

2.

Tensorization
: Let
\{P_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\{Q_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be distributions on a measurable space. Then,

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{i},\prod_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{\mathsf{TV}}(P_{i},Q_{i}).

(600)

Accordingly, using the above two properties we readily obtain that,

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{TV}}({\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{0}},{\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{1}})

\displaystyle=d_{\mathsf{TV}}({\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{0}},\mathbb{E}_{\Gamma}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{1}|\Gamma})

(601)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\Gamma}d_{\mathsf{TV}}({\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{0}},{\mathbb{P}}_{{\cal H}_{1}|\Gamma})

(602)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\Gamma}d_{\mathsf{TV}}\left(\prod_{(i,j)\in\binom{[n]}{2}}\mathsf{Bern}(q),\prod_{(i,j)\in\binom{[n]}{2}\setminus\Gamma}\mathsf{Bern}(q)\times\prod_{(i,j)\in\Gamma}\mathsf{Bern}(p)\right)

(603)

\displaystyle\leq|p-q|\cdot|e(\Gamma)|,

(604)

and thus,
\mathsf{R}^{\star}\geq 1-|p-q|\cdot|e(\Gamma)|
, which concludes the proof.

A.2

Graphs with sub-logarithmic degrees: alternative probabilistic proof

Our main results were proved using a combination of combinatorial and probabilistic approaches. Many of the existing results in the literature, for specific structures, rely on probabilistic arguments only. In this appendix, we present a pure probabilistic proof, which give tight lower bounds for the family of sug-logarithmic degree graphs (i.e.,
d_{\max}(\Gamma)=o(\log(n))
), which might be of independent interest. We prove the following result.

Lemma 16
.

Let
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
be two graphs drawn uniformly and independently at random from
{\cal S}_{\Gamma_{1}}
and
{\cal S}_{\Gamma_{2}}
, respectively. Assume further that
d_{\max}(\Gamma_{1})=O(\log n)
and
|e(\Gamma_{2})|=O(n^{1-\varepsilon})
, for some
\varepsilon>0
. Then, for any constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Gamma_{1}\perp\!\!\!\perp\Gamma_{2}}\left[(1+\lambda^{2})^{c\cdot|e(\Gamma_{1}\cap\Gamma_{2})|}\right]=1+o(1).

(605)

.

. Let us also denote the fixed deterministic versions of

Appendix B
Useful Lemmata

In this section, we record a few well known auxiliary results.

Lemma 18

(Chernoff’s inequality)
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p,q\in(0,1)
such that
p\geq q
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{Binomial}(n,q)\geq pn\right]\leq\exp\left[-n\cdot d_{\mathsf{KL}}(p||q)\right].

(610)

Lemma 19

(Multiplicative Chernoff’s inequality)
.

Suppose
\{S_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are independent random variables taking values in
\{0,1\}
, where
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
S_{n}\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{n}S_{i}
, and denote
\eta\triangleq\mathbb{E}[X]
. Then, for any
0<\delta<1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[S_{n}\leq(1-\delta)\eta\right]\leq\exp\left[-\frac{\delta^{2}\eta}{2}\right].

(611)

Lemma 20

(Bernstein’s inequality)
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\in(0,1)
, and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{Binomial}(n,p)\geq np+t\right]\leq\exp\left[-\frac{t^{2}}{2[np(1-p)+t/3]}\right].

(612)

Appendix C
Failure of Local Subgraph Counts Bounds

One idea to handle the expression in (
95
) is by using Janson’s type bounds. Indeed, note that by using Lemma
2
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\cal H}_{0}}\left[\mathsf{L}(\mathsf{G})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathsf{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}}\lambda^{2|\mathsf{H}^{\prime}|}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\Gamma}\left[\mathsf{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\right]

(613)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathsf{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}}\lambda^{2|\mathsf{H}^{\prime}|}\cdot\frac{{\cal N}(\mathsf{H}^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})}{\left|{\cal S}_{\mathsf{H}}\right|}.

(614)

Now, let
{\cal N}(n,m,\mathsf{H})
be the largest number of copies of
\mathsf{H}
that can be packed in
n
vertices and
m
edges. Clearly
{\cal N}(\mathsf{H},\Gamma)
can be bounded by
{\cal N}\left(|v(\Gamma)|,|e(\Gamma)|,\mathsf{H}\right)
, which received significant attention in the literature. Indeed, many bounds on
{\cal N}(v(\Gamma),e(\Gamma),\mathsf{H})
exist in the literature, e.g.,
[
Alo81
,
JOR04
,
FK98
,
Rad20
,
HMS22
]
, and here we will focus the following recent result from
[
HMS22
]
. Specifically, for every graph
\mathsf{H}
without isolated vertices, and for all
e(\mathsf{H})\leq e(\Gamma)
and
v(\mathsf{H})\leq v(\Gamma)
, we have
[
HMS22
, Theorem 5.4]
,

\displaystyle{\cal N}(v(\Gamma),e(\Gamma),\mathsf{H})\leq[2e(\Gamma)]^{v(\mathsf{H})-\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})}\cdot\min\left\{2e(\Gamma),v(\Gamma)\right\}^{2\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})-v(\mathsf{H})},

(615)

where for a given graph
\mathsf{H}
, the fractional independence number of
\mathsf{H}
, denoted by
\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})
is the largest value of
\sum_{v}\alpha_{v}
over all assignments of weights
\alpha_{v}\in[0,1]
to the vertices of
\mathsf{H}
satisfying the condition
\alpha_{v}+\alpha_{u}\leq 1
, for all edges
(u,v)
of
\mathsf{H}
. It was shown in
[
JOR04
, Lemma A.1]
that if
e(\mathsf{H})>0
then,

\displaystyle 1\leq\frac{v(\mathsf{H})}{2}\leq\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})\leq v(\mathsf{H})-\frac{e(\mathsf{H})}{\Delta(\mathsf{H})}\leq v(\mathsf{H})-1,

(616)

where
\Delta(\mathsf{H})
is the maximum degree in
\mathsf{H}
. Therefore, combining (
614
) and (
615
) we obtain,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{L}(\mathsf{G})\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{\emptyset\neq\mathsf{H}\subseteq\binom{e(\Gamma)}{2}}\lambda^{2|\mathsf{H}|}\frac{[2e(\Gamma)]^{v(\mathsf{H})-\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})}\cdot\min\left\{2e(\Gamma),v(\Gamma)\right\}^{2\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})-v(\mathsf{H})}}{|{\cal S}_{\mathsf{H}}|}

(617)

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{\emptyset\neq\mathsf{H}\subseteq\binom{e(\Gamma)}{2}}\lambda^{2|\mathsf{H}|}\frac{[2e(\Gamma)]^{v(\mathsf{H})-\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})}\cdot\min\left\{2e(\Gamma),v(\Gamma)\right\}^{2\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})-v(\mathsf{H})}}{\binom{n}{|v(\mathsf{H})|}\cdot\frac{|v(\mathsf{H})|!}{|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{H})|}}.

(618)

The goal is now to derive rather sufficient conditions under which the former is bounded. While at first sight, this might seem like a promising approach, it is quite loose. To illustrate this, let us consider the case of planted path in the dense regime; our bounds in this paper show that the detection problem in this case is impossible whenever
|v(\Gamma)|=o(n)
, which is rather an intuitive result. We next analyze the above local Janson-type approach.

Let us begin by recalling from
[
JOR04
]
, that for a path of with
s
edges (and
s+1
vertices) the fractional covering number
\alpha^{\star}
is
(s+1)/2
if
s
is odd, and
(s+2)/2
if
s
is even. We also observe that any subgraph
\mathsf{H}
with
\ell
edges of a path with
k
edges is a disjoint union of
P(H)=m
paths of lengths
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m}
such that
\sum_{i}\ell_{i}=\ell
. Since
|v(\mathsf{H})|\leq k+1
and

|v(\mathsf{H})|=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\overset{\text{length of the $i$'th path}}{\overbrace{\ell_{i}+1}}=m+\ell,

we have

m\leq k-\ell+1.

On the other hand the number of paths
m
will always be at most the number of edges, and therefore
m\leq\ell
.
Immediately from the definition of
\alpha^{\star}
, we observe that for a disjoint union of graphs
\mathsf{H}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{H}_{\ell}
,

\alpha^{\star}\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathsf{H}_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\alpha^{\star}\left(\mathsf{H}_{i}\right).

Combining the above, any subgraph of
\Gamma
, a path of length
k
edges, is a disjoint union of
m
paths of lengths
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m}
, where
1\leq\ell\leq\min(k+1-\ell,\ell)
, and

\frac{\ell}{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\ell_{i}}{2}\leq\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\ell_{i}+2}{2}=m+\frac{\ell}{2}.

We also recall the following result.

Lemma 21
.

[
Fru49
]

Let
\mathsf{G}
be a graph composed as a union of disjoint graphs
\mathsf{G}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\bigcup_{j=1}^{h_{i}}\mathsf{G}_{i,j}
, such that for any
i
,
\mathsf{G}_{i,1}\dots\mathsf{G}_{i,h_{i}}
are isomorphic and for any
i^{\prime}\neq i
and each
j^{\prime}
the graphs
\mathsf{G}_{i,j}
and
\mathsf{G}_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
are not isomorphic. Then:

\left|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{G})\right|=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\left|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{G}_{i,1})\right|^{h_{i}}\cdot h_{i}!

(619)

To wit, the size of the automorphism group of a disjoint union of
m
paths of lengths
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m}
with
\sum_{i}\ell_{i}=\ell
is given by

|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{H})|=2^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}p_{\mathsf{H}}(i)!,

where
p_{\mathsf{H}}(i)
is the number sub-paths with length
i
.

No we can further analyze (
618
):

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{L(G)}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{\emptyset\neq\mathsf{H}\subseteq\binom{e(\Gamma)}{2}}\lambda^{2|\mathsf{H}|}\frac{(2|e(\Gamma)|)^{\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})}}{\binom{n}{|v(\mathsf{H})|}\cdot\frac{|v(\mathsf{H})|!}{|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{H})|}}

(620)

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\min(\ell,k+1-\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{H}\subseteq\binom{e(\Gamma)}{2}\\
(|e(\mathsf{H})|,|P(\mathsf{H})|)=(\ell,m)\end{subarray}}\lambda^{2\ell}\frac{(2k)^{\alpha^{\star}(\mathsf{H})}2^{m}\prod_{i}p_{\mathsf{H}}(i)}{\frac{n!}{(n-m-\ell)!}}

(621)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\min(\ell,k+1-\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{H}\subseteq\binom{e(\Gamma)}{2}\\
(|e(\mathsf{H})|,|P(\mathsf{H})|)=(\ell,m)\end{subarray}}\lambda^{2\ell}(2k)^{\frac{\ell}{2}+m}2^{m}\frac{(n-m-\ell)!}{n!}\prod_{i}p_{\mathsf{H}}(i).

(622)

The most inner sum in the above expression is the same for all subgraphs of the path
\mathsf{H}
with composed from a disjoint union of exactly
m
paths of lengths of
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m}
. Now let us evaluate the inner sum. To that end, we should count over all equivalence classes (under graph isomorphism) representing subgraphs with
\ell
edges and
m
disjoint paths of lengths
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m}
. Next, we consider the size of any such equivalent class, which is exactly the size of
{\cal N}(\mathsf{H},\Gamma)
, for a representing element
\mathsf{H}
. To that end, we begin by observing that a non-empty subgraph of a path is a disjoint union of paths. Let
\mathsf{H}
be such a subgraphs composed of
\ell
edges,
m
disjoint paths, such that for any
1\leq i\leq\ell
, the subgraph
\mathsf{H}
contains exactly
h_{i}
paths of length
i
. We begin by analyzing
{\cal N}(\mathsf{H},\Gamma)
and
{\cal S}(\mathsf{H})
. As we know,

\displaystyle{\cal S}(\mathsf{H})=\binom{n}{|v(\mathsf{H})|}\frac{|v(\mathsf{H})|!}{|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{H})|}=\binom{n}{\ell+m}\frac{(\ell+m)!}{|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{H})|}.

(623)

Thus, we aim to determine
\left|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{H})\right|
. We note any two sub paths of
\mathsf{H}
are isomorphic if and only if they have the same length. We also note that a path as exactly two isomorphisms, the identity and the one that reverses the direction of the path. Using Lemma
21

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{Aut}(\mathsf{H})\right|=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}2^{h_{i}}\cdot h_{i}!=2^{\sum_{i=1}^{m}h_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}h_{i}!=2^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}h_{i}!.

(624)

Next, we turn to evaluate
{\cal N}(\mathsf{H},\Gamma)
, i.e., the number of copies of
\mathsf{H}
in
\Gamma
. We count this number of copies as follows. First, we go over all
m!
orderings of the distinct paths composing
\mathsf{H}
in line, according to their order of appearance in
\Gamma
. We note that by doing so, generate any each order multiple times and we therefore over count since when counting copies of
\mathsf{H}
we do not distinguish between different sub-paths of the same length. Thus, we should normalize by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}h_{i}!},

(625)

which is the number of internal orderings of paths of the same length. In order to count the number of copies, it only remains to go over all possibilities of the number of edges separating between any two distinct sub-paths, which is at least
1
(as the paths are disjoint). Thus, we count the number of ways distribute the renaming
k-(\ell+m-1)
edges between the distinct paths. That is exactly the number ways to distribute
k-(\ell+m-1)
elements in
m+1
bins (we note that edges may be located before the first path and after the last path as well). The number of ways to distribute
q
elements into
d
cells is known to be equal to
\binom{q+d-1}{d-1}
, and therefore we have

\displaystyle\binom{k-(\ell+m-1)+(m+1)-1}{(m+1)-1}=\binom{k-\ell+1}{m}

(626)

possibilities of the remaining edges. Combining all together we have,

\displaystyle{\cal N}(\mathsf{H},\Gamma)=\frac{m!}{\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}h_{i}!}\cdot\binom{k-\ell+1}{m}=\frac{(k-\ell+1)!}{(k-\ell-m+1)!\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}h_{i}!}\leq\frac{\left(k-\ell+1\right)^{m}}{\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}p_{\mathsf{H}}(i)!}.

(627)

Note that different equivalence classes are distinguished by the number of parts of each size, which corresponds to integer partitions of
m
to exactly
\ell
parts. For an integer partition
p
, we denote
p(i)
the number of elements of size
i
in the partition. We combine it with our bound and obtain:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{L(G)}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\min(\ell,k+1-\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{H}\subseteq\binom{e(\Gamma)}{2}\\
(|e(\mathsf{H})|,|P(\mathsf{H})|)=(\ell,m)\end{subarray}}\lambda^{2\ell}(2k)^{\frac{\ell}{2}+m}2^{m}\frac{(n-m-\ell)!}{n!}\prod_{i}p_{\mathsf{H}}(i)

(628)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\min(\ell,k+1-\ell)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{H}\subseteq\binom{e(\Gamma)}{2}\\
(|e(\mathsf{H})|,|P(\mathsf{H})|)=(\ell,m)\end{subarray}}\lambda^{2\ell}(2k)^{\frac{\ell}{2}+m}2^{m}(n-m-l)^{-(m+\ell)}\prod_{i}p_{\mathsf{H}}(i)

(629)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\min(\ell,k+1-\ell)}\sum_{p\in\mathsf{Par}(\ell,m)}{\cal N}(\mathsf{H}_{p},\Gamma)\lambda^{2\ell}(2k)^{\frac{\ell}{2}+m}2^{m}(n-m-l)^{-(m+\ell)}\prod_{i}p_{\mathsf{H}}(i)

(630)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\min(\ell,k+1-\ell)}\sum_{p\in\mathsf{Par}(\ell,m)}(k-\ell+1)^{m}\lambda^{2\ell}(2k)^{\frac{\ell}{2}+m}2^{m}(n-k-1)^{-(m+\ell)}

(631)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\min(\ell,k+1-\ell)}e^{c\sqrt{\ell}}(k-\ell+1)^{m}\lambda^{2\ell}(2k)^{\frac{\ell}{2}+m}2^{m}(n-k-1)^{-(m+\ell)}

(632)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\frac{e^{c\sqrt{\ell}}2^{\frac{3\ell}{2}}k^{\frac{\ell}{2}}}{(n-k-1)^{\ell}}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\left(\frac{2k^{2}}{n-k-1}\right)^{m}

(633)

where

(a)

follows from (
627
) and since
m+\ell\leq k+1
.

(b)

follows from (
).

Assuming that
k
is in the interesting regime
\sqrt{n}\ll k
. we have
\frac{2k^{2}}{n-k-1}\to\infty
and

\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\left(\frac{2k^{2}}{n-k-1}\right)^{m}=(1+o(1))\left(\frac{2k^{2}}{n-k-1}\right)^{\ell}.

(634)

Combining the above with our calculation, we get,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{L(G)}\right\|_{{\cal H}_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 1+(1+o(1))\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\frac{e^{c\sqrt{\ell}}2^{\frac{\ell}{2}}k^{\frac{\ell}{2}}}{(n-k-1)^{\ell}}\left(\frac{2k^{2}}{n-k-1}\right)^{\ell}

(635)

\displaystyle\leq 1+(1+o(1))\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda^{2}C}k^{\frac{5}{2}}}{(n-k-1)^{2}}\right)^{\ell}.

(636)

Thus, the above sum converge when
k\ll n^{\frac{4}{5}}
. This is of course loose, as we already proved that detection is impossible for
k\ll n^{\frac{4}{5}}
.
